{"success": true, "name": "hermes-agent", "description": "Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.", "tags": ["hermes", "setup", "configuration", "multi-agent", "spawning", "cli", "gateway", "development"], "related_skills": ["claude-code", "codex", "opencode"], "content": "---\nname: hermes-agent\ndescription: \"Configure, extend, or contribute to Hermes Agent.\"\nversion: 2.1.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent + Teknium\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [hermes, setup, configuration, multi-agent, spawning, cli, gateway, development]\n    homepage: https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent\n    related_skills: [claude-code, codex, opencode]\n---\n\n# Hermes Agent\n\nHermes Agent is an open-source AI agent framework by Nous Research that runs in your terminal, messaging platforms, and IDEs. It belongs to the same category as Claude Code (Anthropic), Codex (OpenAI), and OpenClaw — autonomous coding and task-execution agents that use tool calling to interact with your system. Hermes works with any LLM provider (OpenRouter, Anthropic, OpenAI, DeepSeek, local models, and 15+ others) and runs on Linux, macOS, and WSL.\n\nWhat makes Hermes different:\n\n- **Self-improving through skills** — Hermes learns from experience by saving reusable procedures as skills. When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. Credential pools rotate across multiple API keys automatically.\n- **Profiles** — run multiple independent Hermes instances with isolated configs, sessions, skills, and memory.\n- **Extensible** — plugins, MCP servers, custom tools, webhook triggers, cron scheduling, and the full Python ecosystem.\n\nPeople use Hermes for software development, research, system administration, data analysis, content creation, home automation, and anything else that benefits from an AI agent with persistent context and full system access.\n\n**This skill helps you work with Hermes Agent effectively** — setting it up, configuring features, spawning additional agent instances, troubleshooting issues, finding the right commands and settings, and understanding how the system works when you need to extend or contribute to it.\n\n**Docs:** https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com/docs/\n\n## Quick Start\n\n``​`bash\n# Install\ncurl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash\n\n# Interactive chat (default)\nhermes\n\n# Single query\nhermes chat -q \"What is the capital of France?\"\n\n# Setup wizard\nhermes setup\n\n# Change model/provider\nhermes model\n\n# Check health\nhermes doctor\n``​`\n\n---\n\n## CLI Reference\n\n### Global Flags\n\n``​`\nhermes [flags] [command]\n\n  --version, -V             Show version\n  --resume, -r SESSION      Resume session by ID or title\n  --continue, -c [NAME]     Resume by name, or most recent session\n  --worktree, -w            Isolated git worktree mode (parallel agents)\n  --skills, -s SKILL        Preload skills (comma-separate or repeat)\n  --profile, -p NAME        Use a named profile\n  --yolo                    Skip dangerous command approval\n  --pass-session-id         Include session ID in system prompt\n``​`\n\nNo subcommand defaults to `chat`.\n\n### Chat\n\n``​`\nhermes chat [flags]\n  -q, --query TEXT          Single query, non-interactive\n  -m, --model MODEL         Model (e.g. anthropic/claude-sonnet-4)\n  -t, --toolsets LIST       Comma-separated toolsets\n  --provider PROVIDER       Force provider (openrouter, anthropic, nous, etc.)\n  -v, --verbose             Verbose output\n  -Q, --quiet               Suppress banner, spinner, tool previews\n  --checkpoints             Enable filesystem checkpoints (/rollback)\n  --source TAG              Session source tag (default: cli)\n``​`\n\n### Configuration\n\n``​`\nhermes setup [section]      Interactive wizard (model|terminal|gateway|tools|agent)\nhermes model                Interactive model/provider picker\nhermes config               View current config\nhermes config edit          Open config.yaml in $EDITOR\nhermes config set KEY VAL   Set a config value\nhermes config path          Print config.yaml path\nhermes config env-path      Print .env path\nhermes config check         Check for missing/outdated config\nhermes config migrate       Update config with new options\nhermes login [--provider P] OAuth login (nous, openai-codex)\nhermes logout               Clear stored auth\nhermes doctor [--fix]       Check dependencies and config\nhermes status [--all]       Show component status\n``​`\n\n### Tools & Skills\n\n``​`\nhermes tools                Interactive tool enable/disable (curses UI)\nhermes tools list           Show all tools and status\nhermes tools enable NAME    Enable a toolset\nhermes tools disable NAME   Disable a toolset\n\nhermes skills list          List installed skills\nhermes skills search QUERY  Search the skills hub\nhermes skills install ID    Install a skill (ID can be a hub identifier OR a direct https://…/SKILL.md URL; pass --name to override when frontmatter has no name)\nhermes skills inspect ID    Preview without installing\nhermes skills config        Enable/disable skills per platform\nhermes skills check         Check for updates\nhermes skills update        Update outdated skills\nhermes skills unins

### assistant

```text
You’re right: it *is* configured in the Hermes config.

What’s broken is the **backend dependency**, not the config:

- `/opt/data/config.yaml` has:
  - `stt.enabled: true`
  - `stt.provider: local`
  - `stt.local.model: base`
- But the local STT provider requires `faster-whisper`, and this runtime does **not** have it installed.
- The gateway log confirms the exact failure:

``​`text
STT provider 'local' configured but unavailable (install faster-whisper or set HERMES_LOCAL_STT_COMMAND)
``​`

I also checked API-key fallbacks, and none are present:

- `GROQ_API_KEY`: missing
- `VOICE_TOOLS_OPENAI_KEY`: missing
- `OPENAI_API_KEY`: missing
- `MISTRAL_API_KEY`: missing

So the answer is: **initial setup configured STT to use local Whisper, but the installed environment no longer has the local Whisper package available.** That can happen after a redeploy/container rebuild, venv replacement, or install that skipped optional voice deps.

Fix options:

``​`bash
/opt/hermes/.venv/bin/pip install faster-whisper
``​`

Then restart the gateway:

``​`bash
hermes gateway restart
``​`

Or configure an API-backed STT provider like Groq/OpenAI/Mistral by adding the key and setting `stt.provider` accordingly.

One extra thing I noticed: `terminal.cwd` is set to `/home/dam57`, but that path doesn’t exist here, which is also causing terminal tool warnings. That’s separate from voice, but should probably be corrected too.
```

{"success": true, "name": "arxiv", "description": "Search arXiv papers by keyword, author, category, or ID.", "tags": ["Research", "Arxiv", "Papers", "Academic", "Science", "API"], "related_skills": ["ocr-and-documents"], "content": "---\nname: arxiv\ndescription: \"Search arXiv papers by keyword, author, category, or ID.\"\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: Hermes Agent\nlicense: MIT\nplatforms: [linux, macos, windows]\nmetadata:\n  hermes:\n    tags: [Research, Arxiv, Papers, Academic, Science, API]\n    related_skills: [ocr-and-documents]\n---\n\n# arXiv Research\n\nSearch and retrieve academic papers from arXiv via their free REST API. No API key, no dependencies — just curl.\n\n## Quick Reference\n\n| Action | Command |\n|--------|---------|\n| Search papers | `curl \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:QUERY&max_results=5\"` |\n| Get specific paper | `curl \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?id_list=[REDACTED_PHONE]\"` |\n| Read abstract (web) | `web_extract(urls=[\"https://arxiv.org/abs/[REDACTED_PHONE]\"])` |\n| Read full paper (PDF) | `web_extract(urls=[\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/[REDACTED_PHONE]\"])` |\n\n## Searching Papers\n\nThe API returns Atom XML. Parse with `grep`/`sed` or pipe through `python3` for clean output.\n\n### Basic search\n\n``​`bash\ncurl -s \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:GRPO+reinforcement+learning&max_results=5\"\n``​`\n\n### Clean output (parse XML to readable format)\n\n``​`bash\ncurl -s \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=all:GRPO+reinforcement+learning&max_results=5&sortBy=submittedDate&sortOrder=descending\" | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, xml.etree.ElementTree as ET\nns = {'a': 'http://www.w3.org/2005/Atom'}\nroot = ET.parse(sys.stdin).getroot()\nfor i, entry in enumerate(root.findall('a:entry', ns)):\n    title = entry.find('a:title', ns).text.strip().replace('\\n', ' ')\n    arxiv_id = entry.find('a:id', ns).text.strip().split('/abs/')[-1]\n    published = entry.find('a:published', ns).text[:10]\n    authors = ', '.join(a.find('a:name', ns).text for a in entry.findall('a:author', ns))\n    summary = entry.find('a:summary', ns).text.strip()[:200]\n    cats = ', '.join(c.get('term') for c in entry.findall('a:category', ns))\n    print(f'{i+1}. [{arxiv_id}] {title}')\n    print(f'   Authors: {authors}')\n    print(f'   Published: {published} | Categories: {cats}')\n    print(f'   Abstract: {summary}...')\n    print(f'   PDF: https://arxiv.org/pdf/{arxiv_id}')\n    print()\n\"\n``​`\n\n## Search Query Syntax\n\n| Prefix | Searches | Example |\n|--------|----------|---------|\n| `all:` | All fields | `all:transformer+attention` |\n| `ti:` | Title | `ti:large+language+models` |\n| `au:` | Author | `au:vaswani` |\n| `abs:` | Abstract | `abs:reinforcement+learning` |\n| `cat:` | Category | `cat:cs.AI` |\n| `co:` | Comment | `co:accepted+NeurIPS` |\n\n### Boolean operators\n\n``​`\n# AND (default when using +)\nsearch_query=all:transformer+attention\n\n# OR\nsearch_query=all:GPT+OR+all:BERT\n\n# AND NOT\nsearch_query=all:language+model+ANDNOT+all:vision\n\n# Exact phrase\nsearch_query=ti:\"chain+of+thought\"\n\n# Combined\nsearch_query=au:hinton+AND+cat:cs.LG\n``​`\n\n## Sort and Pagination\n\n| Parameter | Options |\n|-----------|---------|\n| `sortBy` | `relevance`, `lastUpdatedDate`, `submittedDate` |\n| `sortOrder` | `ascending`, `descending` |\n| `start` | Result offset (0-based) |\n| `max_results` | Number of results (default 10, max 30000) |\n\n``​`bash\n# Latest 10 papers in cs.AI\ncurl -s \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?search_query=cat:cs.AI&sortBy=submittedDate&sortOrder=descending&max_results=10\"\n``​`\n\n## Fetching Specific Papers\n\n``​`bash\n# By arXiv ID\ncurl -s \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?id_list=[REDACTED_PHONE]\"\n\n# Multiple papers\ncurl -s \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?id_list=[REDACTED_PHONE],[REDACTED_PHONE],[REDACTED_PHONE]\"\n``​`\n\n## BibTeX Generation\n\nAfter fetching metadata for a paper, generate a BibTeX entry:\n\n{% raw %}\n``​`bash\ncurl -s \"https://export.arxiv.org/api/query?id_list=[REDACTED_PHONE]\" | python3 -c \"\nimport sys, xml.etree.ElementTree as ET\nns = {'a': 'http://www.w3.org/2005/Atom', 'arxiv': 'http://arxiv.org/schemas/atom'}\nroot = ET.parse(sys.stdin).getroot()\nentry = root.find('a:entry', ns)\nif entry is None: sys.exit('Paper not found')\ntitle = entry.find('a:title', ns).text.strip().replace('\\n', ' ')\nauthors = ' and '.join(a.find('a:name', ns).text for a in entry.findall('a:author', ns))\nyear = entry.find('a:published', ns).text[:4]\nraw_id = entry.find('a:id', ns).text.strip().split('/abs/')[-1]\ncat = entry.find('arxiv:primary_category', ns)\nprimary = cat.get('term') if cat is not None else 'cs.LG'\nlast_name = entry.find('a:author', ns).find('a:name', ns).text.split()[-1]\nprint(f'@article{{{last_name}{year}_{raw_id.replace(\\\".\\\", \\\"\\\")},')\nprint(f'  title     = {{{title}}},')\nprint(f'  author    = {{{authors}}},')\nprint(f'  year      = {{{year}}},')\nprint(f'  eprint    = {{{raw_id}}},')\nprint(f'  archivePrefix = {{arXiv}},')\nprint(f'  primaryClass  = {{{primary}}},')\nprint(f'  url       = {{https://arxiv.org/abs/{raw_id}}}')\nprint('}')\n\"\n``​`\n{% endraw %}\n\n## Reading Paper Content\n\nAfter finding a paper, read it:\n\n``​`\n# Abstract page (fast, metadata + abstract)\nweb_extract(urls=[\"https://arxiv.org/abs/[REDACTED_PHONE]\"])\n\n# Full paper (PDF → markdown via Firecrawl)\nweb_extract(urls=[\"https://arxiv.org/pdf/[REDACTED_PHONE]\"])\n``​`\n\nFor local PDF processing, see the `ocr-and-documents` skill.\n\n## Common Categories\n\n| Category | Field |\n|----------|-------|\n| `cs.AI` | Artificial Intelligence |\n| `cs.CL` | Computation and Language (NLP) |\n| `cs.CV` | Computer Vision |\n| `cs.LG` | Machine Learning |\n| `cs.CR` | Cryptography and Security |\n| `stat.ML` | Machine Learning (Statistics) |\n| `math.OC` | Optimization and Control |\n| `physics.comp-ph` | Computational Physics |\n\nFull list: https://arxiv.

```text
{"text":"Title: The Transformer: the engine behind modern AI.\n\nImagine you are reading a dense paragraph. You do not understand each word in isolation. When you see the word bank, your mind looks across the sentence: river bank, investment bank, memory bank. Meaning comes from relationships. The transformer is a machine built around that simple but profound idea: every word should be able to look at every other word and decide what matters.\n\nThe key paper is Attention Is All You Need, published in 2017 by Vaswani and colleagues at Google. Before it, most language AI read text sequentially, like beads on a string. Recurrent neural networks processed word one, then word two, then word three. That made long-range relationships hard and training slow. The transformer changed the unit of computation from sequence to attention.\n\nHere is the core trick. The model turns each token into numbers. Then for every token it creates three little representations: a query, a key, and a value. The query asks, what am I looking for? The key says, what information do I contain? The value says, if you pay attention to me, here is what you get. The model compares queries and keys across the whole context, produces attention scores, and blends values accordingly.\n\nSo in the sentence, The trophy would not fit in the suitcase because it was too small, the word it needs to know whether it refers to trophy or suitcase. Attention lets it look back, weigh the possibilities, and connect it to suitcase. That is not magic understanding, but it is a powerful statistical mechanism for building context-sensitive meaning.\n\nA transformer layer has several parts. First, self-attention, where tokens exchange information. Second, multiple attention heads, meaning the model can track different relationships at once: grammar, reference, topic, chronology, code structure, and more. Third, a feed-forward network, which transforms and refines each token after it has gathered context. Stack many of these layers, train on huge text, and you get a system that learns reusable patterns: facts, syntax, style, analogy, reasoning traces, programming conventions.\n\nWhy was Ilya Sutskever reportedly so excited? Because the architecture removed a bottleneck. It could train in parallel on modern hardware, scale cleanly with more data and compute, and preserve relationships across long spans. It was not just a better language model component. It was a scaling engine.\n\nGPT-style models use the decoder side of the transformer. During training, they predict the next token again and again. That sounds humble, but prediction forces compression of the world. To predict the next word in a legal brief, a model must learn something about law. To predict code, it must learn syntax and software patterns. To predict an argument, it must learn rhetorical and causal structure.\n\nBy 2026, transformers are still the backbone of frontier LLMs, but they have been extended. Models use mixture-of-experts to activate only parts of a huge network per token. They use long-context attention variants so they can process books, codebases, and multimodal streams. They use retrieval, tools, reinforcement learning, synthetic data, and inference-time reasoning. But underneath, the central operation remains attention: choose what context matters right now.\n\nA useful analogy is an executive briefing room. Every word is a junior analyst. In older systems, analysts passed notes down a line. In a transformer, ev

follow the reference's looping GIF feel: small zoom/bob plus a fade pulse\n    scale = 0.72 + 0.035*math.sin(2*math.pi*t)\n    target_w = int(W*scale)\n    target_h = int(head.size[1] * target_w / head.size[0])\n    h2 = head.resize((target_w, target_h), Image.Resampling.LANCZOS)\n    angle = 1.5*math.sin(2*math.pi*t + 0.5)\n    h2 = h2.rotate(angle, resample=Image.Resampling.BICUBIC, expand=True)\n    # add drop shadow\n    shadow = Image.new('RGBA', h2.size, (0,0,0,0))\n    alpha = h2.getchannel('A')\n    shadow.putalpha(alpha.filter(ImageFilter.GaussianBlur(7)).point(lambda p: int(p*0.35)))\n    x = int((W - h2.size[0])/2 + 4*math.sin(2*math.pi*t+1.2))\n    y = int(H*0.12 + 5*math.sin(2*math.pi*t+2.0))\n    bg.alpha_composite(shadow, (x+4, y+6))\n    bg.alpha_composite(h2, (x, y))\n    # - `y`: how far from the **top edge**
- `w`: how wide the face overlay should be
- `h`: how tall the face overlay should be

- top-left corner = `x=0, y=0`
- right edge = `x=320`
- bottom edge = `y=320`

```text
Yes — use the same “coordinate box” method, but on the **source image crop** instead of the GIF placement.

There are two separate boxes:

1. **Source crop box** — which part of your original image I cut out as the replacement face.
2. **Target overlay box** — where that cut-out face gets placed inside the GIF.

We just adjusted the **target overlay box**:

``​`text
GIF target box:
x=100 → 230
y=20 → 170
``​`

Now to adjust the **face crop from your original image**, describe the crop box on the original image.

Your original source image is:

``​`text
/opt/data/cache/images/img_3d8de553e3a8.jpg
size: 370 × 209
``​`

Current source crop is roughly:

``​`text
Source crop:
x=112 → 242
y=24 → 172
``​`

That means I’m taking this rectangle from the original image:

- left edge: `x=112`
- right edge: `x=242`
- top edge: `y=24`
- bottom edge: `y=172`

## How to tell me adjustments

You can say things like:

- “Crop 10px less on the left”
  - becomes `x=102 → 242`
- “Crop 10px more on the top”
  - becomes `y=34 → 172`
- “Include more hair”
  - lower the top y value, e.g. `y=14`
- “Include less shoulders”
  - raise the bottom y value, e.g. `y=155`
- “Make the crop tighter around his face”
  - increase left x, decrease right x, increase top y, decrease bottom y
- “Move source crop 8px right”
  - add 8 to both x values: `x=120 → 250`

## Current full recipe

``​`text
Source image crop:
x=112 → 242
y=24 → 172

GIF placement:
x=100 → 230
y=20 → 170
``​`

So if the face shape looks wrong, adjust the **source crop**.  
If the face is in the wrong place in the GIF, adjust the **GIF placement**.

Easiest instruction format:

``​`text
Keep GIF placement the same. Change source crop to:
x=___ → ___
y=___ → ___
``​`

Or just say:

``​`text
Use more forehead and less chin
``​`

and I can translate that into crop coordinates.

[DJ Mauch] [Content of hermes-thought-capture-spec.md]:
# Hermes Thought Capture & Synthesis System

## Purpose

Build a thought capture and synthesis system inside Hermes. I capture atomic thoughts via Telegram throughout the day. Hermes rolls them into daily files, then synthesizes weekly, monthly, and quarterly. The system compounds — the value lives in the synthesis layers and the retrieval interface.

Treat this as a build spec. Implement the full system; do not phase delivery. The synthesis prompts are expected to be revised after the first 2–3 weeklies — that is the system working as designed, not phasing.

---

## Architecture

- **Capture surface:** dedicated Telegram chat between me and Hermes (the `thoughts` channel). No other traffic in this chat.
- **Storage:** Git repo `thoughts-repo`. Plain markdown files. Commit after every write.
- **Synthesis:** scheduled jobs at daily / weekly / monthly / quarterly cadences.
- **Retrieval:** natural language queries via Hermes, backed by grep + embeddings (use the existing Central Brain).
- **Timezone:** all scheduling in America/New_York.
- **Privacy:** repo is local-first. If pushed to remote, push to a private GitHub repo only; encrypt sensitive directories at rest with `git-crypt` (`daily/`, `weekly/`, `monthly/`, `quarterly/`, `attachments/`). No cloud backups outside the encrypted remote. **This decision is documented in the README before the first commit.**

---

## Repo structure

``​`
thoughts-repo/
  daily/YYYY/MM/YYYY-MM-DD.md
  weekly/YYYY/YYYY-Www.md         # ISO week
  monthly/YYYY/YYYY-MM.md
  quarterly/YYYY/YYYY-Qn.md
  attachments/YYYY/MM/DD/         # voice files, images, fetched URLs
  prompts/
    weekly.md
    monthly.md
    quarterly.md
  config/
    vocab.txt                     # proper nouns for Whisper initial_prompt
    corrections.json              # regex map for known mishearings
  README.md
``​`

---

## Capture (real-time, on every Telegram message)

When a message arrives in the `thoughts` channel:

- **Text** → append to current day's daily file with `HH:MM ET` timestamp.
- **Voice note** → transcribe via Whisper, passing `config/vocab.txt` as `initial_prompt`, then run `config/corrections.json` regex pass. Append transcript with `[voice]` prefix. Preserve audio at `attachments/YYYY/MM/DD/HHMMSS.ogg`.
- **Image / screenshot** → OCR. Append extracted text with `[image]` prefix. Preserve original in `attachments/`.
- **URL** → fetch, write 2–3 sentence summary. Append URL + summary with `[link]` prefix.
- **Forwarded message** → preserve source attribution if available, append text.

### Intensity convention

If a message begins with `!`, synthesis layers should treat it as a higher-weight entry. This is the ONLY allowed convention. It is not a tag and it is not a category — it is an intensity flag. Synthesis prompts must reference and honor this.

### Daily file format

``​`markdown
# [REDACTED_PHONE] (Thursday)

## 07:42
Raw thought as written.

## 08:15 [voice]
Transcript of voice note.

## 09:03 [link] https://example.com/article
Two-sentence summary of what was at the URL.

## 09:04 !
Follow-up thought I'm flagging as significant.
``​`

### Rules

- Append-only. Never edit prior entries.
- File created lazily on first message of the day.
- No tagging, no categorization, no workstream labels at capture. Structure emerges at synthesis.
- **Corrections allowed via follow-up entries only.** If Whisper hallucinated something or I want to correct an earlier entry, I send a new message starting with `correction:` referencing the time. The original stays untouched.
- Commit to Git after each message (batched every 60s acceptable if commit volume becomes an issue).

---

## Vocabulary & transcription quality

`config/vocab.txt` holds proper nouns and frequently-mistranscribed terms. Initial seed includes: Pura Vida, ICONIQ, Starlight 13, Riyadh Air, MFC, Flow MENA, Hermes, Chief, Sandra, JAWS Ventures, BMAD. Extend as needed.

After every weekly synthesis, scan the past week's `[voice]` entries for entities that appear in cleaned text but not in `vocab.txt`, and surface them for me to add. Maintain `corrections.json` for systematic mishearings (e.g., `"Pure Vida"` → `"Pura Vida"`).

This is not optional. Without it the corpus rots and grep stops working three months in.

---

## Daily rollup (00:05 ET)

- Close out yesterday's file. Append a footer: total entries, voice count, link count, intensity-flagged count, total estimated word count.
- Commit with message `daily: YYYY-MM-DD (N entries)`.
- No transformation of content.

---

## Morning replay (07:00 ET)

- Send yesterday's complete daily file back to me in the `thoughts` channel as a single message (split if >4000 chars).
- No synthesis, no commentary. Just the raw dump.
- Purpose: I see my own patterns before any LLM tells me what they are.

---

## Weekly synthesis (Monday 05:30 ET, for prior Mon–Sun)

- Run BEFORE the 07:00 morning replay so I see the weekly alongside Sunday's raw dump.
- Read the previous 7 daily files (Monday through Sunday — Sunday's full capture is included because we run Monday morning, not Sunday afternoon).
- Apply the weekly synthesis prompt at `prompts/weekly.md`.
- Write to `weekly/YYYY/YYYY-Www.md`.
- Deliver to Telegram `thoughts` channel.
- Commit.

---

## Monthly synthesis (1st of month, 09:00 ET)

- **Load order matters.** Read all daily files from the prior month FIRST — this is the primary source. THEN read the 4–5 weekly syntheses as cross-check only.
- Apply `prompts/monthly.md`.
- Write to `monthly/YYYY/YYYY-MM.md`.
- Deliver: Telegram message with one-paragraph preview + link to the file; full file body in email.
- Commit.

Rationale: loading weeklies first causes the model to roll them up. Loading dailies firs

[truncated 8584 chars]
```

When it solves a complex problem, discovers a workflow, or gets corrected, it can persist that knowledge as a skill document that loads into future sessions. Skills accumulate over time, making the agent better at your specific tasks and environment.\n- **Persistent memory across sessions** — remembers who you are, your preferences, environment details, and lessons learned. Pluggable memory backends (built-in, Honcho, Mem0, and more) let you choose how memory works.\n- **Multi-platform gateway** — the same agent runs on Telegram, Discord, Slack, WhatsApp, Signal, Matrix, Email, and 10+ other platforms with full tool access, not just chat.\n- **Provider-agnostic** — swap models and providers mid-workflow without changing anything else. {"content":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\nimport subprocess, sys\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom zoneinfo import ZoneInfo\nkind = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv)>1 else 'weekly'\nnow = datetime.now(ZoneInfo('America/New_York'))\nif kind == 'weekly' and not (now.weekday() == 0 and now.hour == 5 and now.minute == 30):\n    sys.exit(0)\nif kind == 'monthly' and not (now.day == 1 and now.hour == 9 and now.minute == 0):\n    sys.exit(0)\nif kind == 'quarterly' and not (now.day == 1 and now.month in (1,4,7,10) and now.hour == 9 and now.minute == 0):\n    sys.exit(0)\ncp = subprocess.run(['/opt/data/scripts/thoughts_system.py','synthesize',kind], text=True, capture_output=True, timeout=360)\nif cp.returncode != 0:\n    print(f'Thoughts {kind} synthesis failed:\\n' + (cp.stderr or cp.stdout)); sys.exit(cp.returncode)\nout = cp.stdout.strip()\nif kind == 'weekly':\n    print(out)\nelif kind == 'monthly':\n    print('Monthly thoughts synthesis is ready.\\n\\n' + out[:1200] + ('\\n\\n[truncated preview]' if len(out)>1200 else ''))\nelif kind == 'quarterly':\n    print('Quarterly thoughts synthesis is ready.\\n\\n' + out[:1200] + ('\\n\\n[truncated preview]' if len(out)>1200 else ''))\n","path":"/opt/data/scripts/thoughts_cron_synthesize.py"}
```

```text
Implemented the Hermes Thought Capture & Synthesis system.

## Shipped

- Created local repo:
  - `/opt/data/thoughts-repo`
- Added required structure:
  - `daily/`
  - `weekly/`
  - `monthly/`
  - `quarterly/`
  - `attachments/`
  - `prompts/`
  - `config/vocab.txt`
  - `config/corrections.json`
  - `README.md`
- README privacy decision is documented before first repo commit.
- Seeded vocab:
  - Pura Vida, ICONIQ, Starlight 13, Riyadh Air, MFC, Flow MENA, Hermes, Chief, Sandra, JAWS Ventures, BMAD
- Added synthesis prompts exactly from your spec.
- Git initialized and commits after writes.

## Capture system

Main script:

``​`text
/opt/data/scripts/thoughts_system.py
``​`

Supports:

- text capture
- voice capture with Whisper + `vocab.txt` initial prompt + `corrections.json`
- image preservation + OCR placeholder/fallback
- URL capture + fetched summary
- correction follow-ups as append-only entries
- `!` intensity flag preservation
- retrieval queries with date/time excerpts
- daily footer rollup
- weekly/monthly/quarterly synthesis

## Telegram integration

I wired the gateway so the dedicated Telegram thoughts channel bypasses the normal LLM conversation and writes directly to the thoughts repo.

Configured thoughts channel as:

``​`yaml
thoughts:
  enabled: true
  repo_path: /opt/data/thoughts-repo
  timezone: America/New_York
  telegram:
    chat_id: '-[REDACTED_PHONE]'
    thread_id: '6'
``​`

That maps to:

``​`text
Chief Group - Hermes / topic 6 / Daily Brain Dump
``​`

Gateway integration file:

``​`text
/opt/hermes/gateway/thoughts_integration.py
``​`

Gateway hook patched in:

``​`text
/opt/hermes/gateway/platforms/base.py
``​`

## Scheduled jobs

Created these cron jobs, delivering to topic 6 / Daily Brain Dump:

- Daily rollup
  - Job: `6399898065a4`
  - Runs at 00:05 ET
- Morning replay
  - Job: `233ac067b175`
  - Runs at 07:00 ET
- Weekly synthesis
  - Job: `0defdc100e19`
  - Runs Monday 05:30 ET
- Monthly synthesis
  - Job: `5769c212e559`
  - Runs 1st of month 09:00 ET
- Quarterly synthesis
  - Job: `e957cb0a7b26`
  - Runs first day of new quarter 09:00 ET

Because Hermes cron uses UTC cron expressions, I scheduled both DST and standard-time UTC hours and added ET guards inside the scripts, so execution stays pinned to America/New_York.

## Verification done

- Repo init verified.
- Git commits verified.
- YAML config verified.
- Python compile checks passed for:
  - `thoughts_system.py`
  - cron wrappers
  - gateway integration
  - patched gateway base adapter
- Simulated Telegram event successfully routed through thought capture integration.

## Important activation note

The gateway process needs a restart for the new capture hook to load, because the running gateway process already imported `base.py`.

Once restarted, messages sent in **Daily Brain Dump / topic 6** should be captured automatically.

## Constraints / remaining external dependencies

- Email delivery for monthly/quarterly is constrained by your existing Google security policy: outbound Gmail send is blocked. I did not override that.
- Quarterly Google Calendar review block is implemented via the local Google wrapper, without attendees/notifications, respecting the policy.
- I did not configure a remote GitHub repo or `git-crypt` because the spec says local-first unless pushed. No remote was created.
- Semantic retrieval uses grep/exact matching now. I did not find an existing “Central Brain” embeddings service exposed on this machine, so the retrieval interface is ready for that backend once its local API/path is available.
```
